        <p>A local agency must initiate an administrative fraud disqualification hearing for individuals accused of wrongfully obtaining assistance or intentional program violations, in lieu of a criminal action when it has not been pursued, in the Minnesota family investment program and any affiliated program to include the diversionary work program and the work participation cash benefit program, child care assistance programs, and the Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP). The Department of Children, Youth, and Families, in lieu of a local agency, may initiate an administrative fraud disqualification hearing when the state agency is directly responsible for administration or investigation of the program for which benefits were wrongfully obtained. The hearing is subject to the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.20">142A.20</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.0451">256.0451</a> and the requirements in Code of Federal Regulations, title 7, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/273.16">273.16</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.27.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.43.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 43</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.47.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 47</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.28">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.28 RECOVERY OF MONEY; APPORTIONMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.28.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Retention rates.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When an assistance recovery amount is collected and posted by a county agency under the provisions governing public assistance programs, the county may keep one-half of the recovery made by the county agency using any method other than recoupment.</p>
          <p>(b) This section does not apply to recoveries by the attorney general's office or child support collections. In the Supplemental Nutrition Assistance Program (SNAP), the nonfederal share of recoveries in the federal tax offset program only will be divided equally between the state agency and the involved county agency.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.28.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Retention rates for AFDC and MFIP.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When an assistance recovery amount is collected and posted by a county agency under the provisions governing the aid to families with dependent children program formerly codified in 1996 in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.72">256.72</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.87">256.87 </a>or MFIP under chapter 142G, the commissioner shall reimburse the county agency from the proceeds of the recovery using the applicable rate specified in paragraph (b) or (c).</p>
          <p>(b) For recoveries of overpayments made on or before September 30, 1996, from the aid to families with dependent children program including the emergency assistance program, the commissioner shall reimburse the county agency at a rate of one-quarter of the recovery made by any method other than recoupment.</p>
          <p>(c) For recoveries of overpayments made after September 30, 1996, from the aid to families with dependent children program including the emergency assistance program and programs funded in whole or in part by the temporary assistance to needy families program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142G.03#stat.142G.03.2">142G.03, subdivision 2</a>, and recoveries of nonfederally funded food assistance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142G.11">142G.11</a>, the commissioner shall reimburse the county agency at a rate of one-quarter of the recovery made by any method other than recoupment.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.28.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">
          <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.44.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 44</a>
        </p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.29">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.29 WORK REPORTING SYSTEM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.29.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Definitions.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The definitions in this subdivision apply to this section.</p>
          <p>(b) "Date of hiring" means the earlier of: (1) the first day for which an employee is owed compensation by an employer; or (2) the first day that an employee reports to work or performs labor or services for an employer.</p>
          <p>(c) "Earnings" means payment owed by an employer for labor or services rendered by an employee.</p>
          <p>(d) "Employee" means a person who resides or works in Minnesota, performs services for compensation, in whatever form, for an employer and satisfies the criteria of an employee under chapter 24 of the Internal Revenue Code. Employee does not include:</p>
          <p>(1) persons hired for domestic service in the private home of the employer, as defined in the Federal Tax Code; or</p>
          <p>(2) an employee of the federal or state agency performing intelligence or counterintelligence functions, if the head of such agency has determined that reporting according to this law would endanger the safety of the employee or compromise an ongoing investigation or intelligence mission.</p>
          <p>(e) "Employer" means a person or entity located or doing business in this state that employs one or more employees for payment, and satisfies the criteria of an employer under chapter 24 of the Internal Revenue Code. Employer includes a labor organization as defined in paragraph (g). Employer also includes the state, political or other governmental subdivisions of the state, and the federal government.</p>
          <p>(f) "Hiring" means engaging a person to perform services for compensation and includes the reemploying or return to work of any previous employee who was laid off, furloughed, separated, granted a leave without pay, or terminated from employment when a period of 60 days elapses from the date of layoff, furlough, separation, leave, or termination to the date of the person's return to work.</p>
          <p>(g) "Labor organization" means entities located or doing business in this state that meet the criteria of labor organization under section 2(5) of the National Labor Relations Act. This includes any entity, that may also be known as a hiring hall, used to carry out requirements described in chapter 7 of the National Labor Relations Act.</p>
          <p>(h) "Payor" means a person or entity located or doing business in Minnesota who pays money to an independent contractor according to an agreement for the performance of services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.29.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Work reporting system established.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of children, youth, and families shall establish a centralized work reporting system for the purpose of receiving and maintaining information from employers on newly hired or rehired employees. The commissioner of children, youth, and families shall take reasonable steps to inform the state's employers of the requirements of this section and the acceptable processes by which employers can comply with the requirements of this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.29.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Duty to report.</span></h2>
          <p>Employers doing business in this state shall report to the commissioner of children, youth, and families the hiring of any employee who resides or works in this state to whom the employer anticipates paying earnings. Employers shall submit reports required under this subdivision within 20 calendar days of the date of hiring of the employee.</p>
          <p>Employers are not required to report the hiring of any person who will be employed for less than two months' duration; and will have gross earnings less than $250 per month.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.29.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Means to report.</span></h2>
          <p>Employers may report by delivering, mailing, or telefaxing a copy of the employee's federal W-4 form or W-9 form or any other document that contains the required information, submitting electronic media in a compatible format, toll-free telecommunication, or other means authorized by the commissioner of children, youth, and families that will result in timely reporting.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.29.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Report contents.</span></h2>
          <p>Reports required under this section must contain all the information required by federal law.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.29.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Sanctions.</span></h2>
          <p>If an employer fails to report under this section, the commissioner of children, youth, and families, by certified mail, shall send the employer a written notice of noncompliance requesting that the employer comply with the reporting requirements of this section. The notice of noncompliance must explain the reporting procedure under this section and advise the employer of the penalty for noncompliance. An employer who has received a notice of noncompliance and later incurs a second violation is subject to a civil penalty of $25 for each intentionally unreported employee. An employer who has received a notice of noncompliance is subject to a civil penalty of $500 for each intentionally unreported employee, if noncompliance is the result of a conspiracy between an employer and an employee not to supply the required report or to supply a false or incomplete report. These penalties may be imposed and collected by the commissioner of children, youth, and families. An employer who has been served with a notice of noncompliance and incurs a second or subsequent violation resulting in a civil penalty, has the right to a contested case hearing under chapter 14. An employer has 20 days from the date of service of the notice, to file a request for a contested case hearing with the commissioner. The order of the administrative law judge constitutes the final decision in the case.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.29.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Access to data.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of children, youth, and families shall retain the information reported to the work reporting system for a period of six months. Data in the work reporting system may be disclosed to the commissioner of children, youth, and families; the public authority responsible for child support enforcement; federal agencies; state and local agencies of other states for the purposes of enforcing state and federal laws governing child support; and agencies responsible for the administration of programs under title IV-A of the Social Security Act, the Department of Employment and Economic Development, and the Department of Labor and Industry.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.29.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Authority to contract.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may contract for services to carry out this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.29.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Independent contractors.</span></h2>
          <p>The state and all political subdivisions of the state, when acting in the capacity of an employer, shall report the hiring of any person as an independent contractor to the centralized work reporting system in the same manner as the hiring of an employee is reported.</p>
          <p>Other payors may report independent contractors to whom they make payments that require the filing of a 1099-MISC report. Payors reporting independent contractors shall report by use of the same means and provide the same information required under subdivisions 4 and 5. The commissioner of children, youth, and families shall establish procedures for payors reporting under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.29.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Use of work reporting system information in determining eligibility for public assistance programs.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of children, youth, and families is authorized to use information from the work reporting system to determine eligibility for applicants and recipients of public assistance programs administered by the Department of Children, Youth, and Families. Data including names, dates of birth, and Social Security numbers of people applying for or receiving public assistance benefits will be compared to the work reporting system information to determine if applicants or recipients of public assistance are employed. County agencies will be notified of discrepancies in information obtained from the work reporting system.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.29.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Action on information.</span></h2>
          <p>Upon receipt of the discrepant information, county agencies will notify clients of the information and request verification of employment status and earnings. County agencies must attempt to resolve the discrepancy within 45 days of receipt of the information.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.29.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Client notification.</span></h2>
          <p>Persons applying for public assistance programs administered by the Department of Children, Youth, and Families will be notified at the time of application that data including their name, date of birth, and Social Security number will be shared with the work reporting system to determine possible employment. All current public assistance recipients will be notified of this provision prior to its implementation.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.29.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/257/#laws.1.16.0">1995 c 257 art 1 s 16</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/203/#laws.6.15.0">1997 c 203 art 6 s 15</a>-20; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/245/#laws.1.10.0">1997 c 245 art 1 s 10</a>; art 3 s 7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2012/0/216/#laws.1.3.0">2012 c 216 art 1 s 3</a>,4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.86.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 86</a>,96; art 8 s 7,70; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.30">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.30 CHILD SUPPORT OBLIGOR COMMUNITY SERVICE WORK EXPERIENCE PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.30.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Authorization.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of children, youth, and families may contract with a county that operates a community work experience program or a judicial district Department of Corrections that operates a community work experience program to include child support obligors who are physically able to work and fail to pay child support as participants in the community work experience program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.30.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Limitations.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except as provided in paragraph (f), a person ordered to participate in a work program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/518A.72">518A.72 </a> shall do so if services are available.</p>
          <p>(b) A person may not be required to participate for more than 32 hours per week in the program under this section.</p>
          <p>(c) A person may not be required to participate for more than six weeks for each finding of contempt.</p>
          <p>(d) If a person is required by a governmental entity to participate in another work or training program, the person may not be required to participate in a program under this section in a week for more than 32 hours minus the number of hours the person is required to participate in the other work or training program in that week.</p>
          <p>(e) If a person is employed, the person may not be required to participate in a program under this section in a week for more than 80 percent of the difference between 40 hours and the number of hours actually worked in the unsubsidized job during that week, to a maximum of 32 hours.</p>
          <p>(f) A person who works an average of 32 hours or more per week in an unsubsidized job is not required to participate in a program under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.30.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Notice to court.</span></h2>
          <p>If a person does not complete six weeks of participation in a program under this section, the county operating the program shall inform the court administrator, by affidavit, of that noncompletion.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.30.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Injury protection for work experience participants.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) This subdivision applies to payment of any claims resulting from an alleged injury or death of a child support obligor participating in a community work experience program established and operated by a county or a judicial district department of corrections under this section.</p>
          <p>(b) Claims that are subject to this section must be investigated by the county agency responsible for supervising the work to determine whether the claimed injury occurred, whether the claimed medical expenses are reasonable, and whether the loss is covered by the claimant's insurance. If insurance coverage is established, the county agency shall submit the claim to the appropriate insurance entity for payment. The investigating county agency shall submit all valid claims, in the amount net of any insurance payments, to the commissioner of children, youth, and families.</p>
          <p>(c) The commissioner of children, youth, and families shall submit all claims for impairment compensation to the commissioner of labor and industry. The commissioner of labor and industry shall review all submitted claims and recommend to the commissioner of children, youth, and families an amount of compensation comparable to what would be provided under the impairment compensation schedule of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.101#stat.176.101.2a">176.101, subdivision 2a</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner of children, youth, and families shall approve a claim of $1,000 or less for payment if appropriated funds are available, if the county agency responsible for supervising the work has made the determinations required by this section, and if the work program was operated in compliance with the safety provisions of this section. The commissioner shall pay the portion of an approved claim of $1,000 or less that is not covered by the claimant's insurance within three months of the date of submission. On or before February 1 of each year, the commissioner shall submit to the appropriate committees of the senate and the house of representatives a list of claims of $1,000 or less paid during the preceding calendar year and shall be reimbursed by legislative appropriation for any claims that exceed the original appropriation provided to the commissioner to operate this program. Unspent money from this appropriation carries over to the second year of the biennium, and any unspent money remaining at the end of the second year must be returned to the general fund. On or before February 1 of each year, the commissioner shall submit to the appropriate committees of the senate and the house of representatives a list of claims in excess of $1,000 and a list of claims of $1,000 or less that were submitted to but not paid by the commissioner of children, youth, and families, together with any recommendations of appropriate compensation. These claims shall be heard and determined by the appropriate committees of the senate and house of representatives and, if approved, paid under the legislative claims procedure.</p>
          <p>(e) Compensation paid under this section is limited to reimbursement for reasonable medical expenses and impairment compensation for disability in like amounts as allowed in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/176.101#stat.176.101.2a">176.101, subdivision 2a</a>. Compensation for injuries resulting in death shall include reasonable medical expenses and burial expenses in addition to payment to the participant's estate in an amount not to exceed the limits set forth in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/466.04">466.04</a>. Compensation may not be paid under this section for pain and suffering, lost wages, or other benefits provided in chapter 176. Payments made under this section must be reduced by any proceeds received by the claimant from any insurance policy covering the loss. For the purposes of this section, "insurance policy" does not include the medical assistance program authorized under chapter 256B.</p>
          <p>(f) The procedure established by this section is exclusive of all other legal, equitable, and statutory remedies against the state, its political subdivisions, or employees of the state or its political subdivisions. The claimant may not seek damages from any state or county insurance policy or self-insurance program.</p>
          <p>(g) A claim is not valid for purposes of this subdivision if the local agency responsible for supervising the work cannot verify to the commissioner of children, youth, and families:</p>
          <p>(1) that appropriate safety training and information is provided to all persons being supervised by the agency under this subdivision; and</p>
          <p>(2) that all programs involving work by those persons comply with federal Occupational Safety and Health Administration and state Department of Labor and Industry safety standards.</p>
          <p>A claim that is not valid because of failure to verify safety training or compliance with safety standards may not be paid by the commissioner of children, youth, and families or through the legislative claims process and must be heard, decided, and paid, if appropriate, by the local government unit responsible for supervising the work of the claimant.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.30.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Transportation expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>A county shall reimburse a person for reasonable transportation costs incurred because of participation in a program under this section, up to a maximum of $25 per month.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.30.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Payment to county.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall pay a county $200 for each person who participates in the program under this section in that county. The county is responsible for any additional costs of the program.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.30.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/0/257/#laws.1.15.0">1995 c 257 art 1 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/164/#laws.0.29.0">2005 c 164 s 29</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/1/7/#laws.0.28.0">1Sp2005 c 7 s 28</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2016/0/158/#laws.1.108.0">2016 c 158 art 1 s 108</a>; art 2 s 73; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; art 8 s 70; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.40">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.40 LEGAL REFERRAL AND ASSISTANCE GRANTS.</h1>
        <p>(a) The commissioner shall award grants to one or more nonprofit programs that provide legal services based on indigency to provide legal services to individuals with emergency medical conditions or chronic health conditions who are not currently eligible for medical assistance or other public health care programs based on their legal status, but who may meet eligibility requirements with legal assistance.</p>
        <p>(b) The grantees, in collaboration with hospitals and safety net providers, shall provide referral assistance to connect individuals identified in paragraph (a) with alternative resources and services to assist in meeting their health care needs.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.40.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.11.18.0">2015 c 71 art 11 s 18;</a> <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.41">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.41 CITATION.</h1>
        <p>Sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.411">142A.411</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.415">142A.415</a> may be cited as the "Children's Trust Fund for the Prevention of Child Abuse Act."</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.41.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/423/#laws.0.1.0">1986 c 423 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.8.25.0">1991 c 292 art 8 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/98/#laws.1.24.0">2005 c 98 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.411">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.411 DEFINITIONS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.411.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Applicability.</span></h2>
          <p>The definitions in this section apply to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.41">142A.41</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.416">142A.416</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.226#stat.144.226.3">144.226, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.411.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Act.</span></h2>
          <p>"Act" means the Children's Trust Fund for the Prevention of Child Abuse Act.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.411.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Child.</span></h2>
          <p>"Child" means a person under 18 years of age.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.411.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Child abuse.</span></h2>
          <p>"Child abuse" means sexual abuse, neglect, or physical abuse as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260E.03">260E.03, subdivisions </a>15, 18, and 20.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.411.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>"Commissioner" means the commissioner of children, youth, and families.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.411.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Local council.</span></h2>
          <p>"Local council" means a child abuse prevention council established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.413">142A.413</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.411.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Prevention program.</span></h2>
          <p>"Prevention program" means a system that directly provides primary or secondary child abuse prevention services to a child, parent or prospective parent, guardian, or professional who works regularly with children, and may also include a research program related to the prevention of child abuse.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.411.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Primary prevention.</span></h2>
          <p>"Primary prevention" means a program or service designed to promote the general welfare of children and families.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.411.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Secondary prevention.</span></h2>
          <p>"Secondary prevention" means a program or service designed to prevent abuse of children who are in circumstances where there is a high risk that abuse will occur.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.411.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Tertiary prevention.</span></h2>
          <p>"Tertiary prevention" means a program or service provided after child abuse has occurred that is designed to prevent its recurrence.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.411.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Trust fund.</span></h2>
          <p>"Trust fund" means the children's trust fund for the prevention of child abuse established under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.412">142A.412</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.411.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/423/#laws.0.2.0">1986 c 423 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.8.8.0">1991 c 292 art 8 s 8</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2002/0/379/#laws.1.37.0">2002 c 379 art 1 s 37</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/98/#laws.1.24.0">2005 c 98 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.1.126.0">2015 c 71 art 1 s 126</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.8.101.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 8 s 101</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.87.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 87</a>,96; art 8 s 70; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.412">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.412 TRUST FUND.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.412.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Creation of trust fund.</span></h2>
          <p>A children's trust fund for the prevention of child abuse is established as an account in the state treasury. The commissioner of management and budget shall credit to the trust fund all amounts received under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.415">142A.415</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.226#stat.144.226.3">144.226, subdivision 3</a>, and shall ensure that trust fund money is invested under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/11A.25">11A.25</a>. All money earned by the trust fund must be credited to the trust fund. The trust fund earns its proportionate share of the total annual state investment income.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.412.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Availability of funds for disbursement.</span></h2>
          <p>Until the total amount of assets in the trust fund exceeds $20,000,000, not more than 60 percent of the gross amount contributed to the trust fund each year under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.226#stat.144.226.3">144.226, subdivision 3</a>, plus 100 percent of all earnings credited to the trust fund the previous fiscal year, are available for disbursement. When the commissioner of management and budget certifies that the assets in the trust fund exceed $20,000,000, only the annual earnings and the funds received under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.415">142A.415</a> that are credited to the trust fund are available for disbursement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.412.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Exception.</span></h2>
          <p>Notwithstanding subdivision 2, money received under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.415">142A.415</a> may be disbursed in its entirety. This money must not be taken into account when calculating the annual contributions to the trust fund under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.412.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Authority to disburse funds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may disburse trust fund money to any public or private nonprofit agency to fund a child abuse prevention program. State funds appropriated for child maltreatment prevention grants may be transferred to the children's trust fund special revenue account and are available to carry out this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.412.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Plan for disbursement of funds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall develop a plan to disburse money from the trust fund. The plan must ensure that all geographic areas of the state have an equal opportunity to establish prevention programs and receive trust fund money.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.412.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Operational costs.</span></h2>
          <p>$120,000 each year is appropriated from the children's trust fund to the special revenue fund for administration and indirect costs of the children's trust fund program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.412.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Responsibilities of commissioner.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) provide for the coordination and exchange of information on the establishment and maintenance of prevention programs;</p>
          <p>(2) develop and publish criteria for receiving trust fund money by prevention programs;</p>
          <p>(3) review, approve, and monitor the spending of trust fund money by prevention programs;</p>
          <p>(4) provide statewide educational and public informational seminars to develop public awareness on preventing child abuse; to encourage professional persons and groups to recognize instances of child abuse and work to prevent them; to make information on child abuse prevention available to the public and to organizations and agencies; and to encourage the development of prevention programs, including programs that provide support for adolescent parents, fathering education programs, and other prevention activities designed to prevent teen pregnancy;</p>
          <p>(5) establish a procedure for an annual, internal evaluation of the functions, responsibilities, and performance of the commissioner in carrying out sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.41">142A.41</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.416">142A.416</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.226#stat.144.226.3">144.226, subdivision 3</a>;</p>
          <p>(6) provide technical assistance to local councils and agencies working in the area of child abuse prevention; and</p>
          <p>(7) accept and review grant applications.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner shall recommend to the governor changes in state programs, statutes, policies, budgets, and standards that will reduce the problems of child abuse, improve coordination among state agencies that provide prevention services, and improve the condition of children, parents, or guardians in need of prevention program services.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.412.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/423/#laws.0.3.0">1986 c 423 s 3</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.8.25.0">1991 c 292 art 8 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/423/#laws.0.4.0">1986 c 423 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/358/#laws.0.113.0">1987 c 358 s 113</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/1/4/#laws.2.6.0">1Sp1987 c 4 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1988/0/629/#laws.0.57.0">1988 c 629 s 57</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1989/0/119/#laws.0.1.0">1989 c 119 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.8.9.0">1991 c 292 art 8 s 9</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1992/0/515/#laws.0.2.0">1992 c 515 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1993/1/1/#laws.3.31.0">1Sp1993 c 1 art 3 s 31</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/7/#laws.2.8.0">1997 c 7 art 2 s 8</a>,9; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/162/#laws.2.3.0">1997 c 162 art 2 s 3</a>-5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2001/1/3/#laws.2.1.0">1Sp2001 c 3 art 2 s 1</a>-4,17; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/98/#laws.1.24.0">2005 c 98 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2009/0/101/#laws.2.109.0">2009 c 101 art 2 s 109</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.88.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 88</a>,96; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.413">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.413 LOCAL CHILD ABUSE PREVENTION COUNCILS.</h1>
        <p>A child abuse prevention council may be established in any county or group of counties that was eligible to receive funds under Minnesota Statutes 1986, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/145.917">145.917</a>, as of January 1, 1986. A council organized in such a county or group of counties shall be authorized by the commissioner to review programs seeking trust fund money on finding that the council meets the criteria in this section:</p>
        <p>(a) The council has submitted a plan for the prevention of child abuse that includes a rank ordering of needed programs and services, assesses the need for additional programs or services, and demonstrates that standards and procedures have been established to ensure that funds will be distributed and used according to sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.41">142A.41 </a>to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.416">142A.416</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.226#stat.144.226.3">144.226, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        <p>(b) A single-county council shall consist of:</p>
        <p>(1) a minimum of nine members with the majority consisting of members from the community-at-large who do not represent service-providing agencies. These members shall represent the demographic and geographic composition of the county and, to the extent possible, represent the following groups: parents, businesses, racial and ethnic minority communities, and the faith communities; and</p>
        <p>(2) if necessary, enough additional members with knowledge in the area of child abuse prevention so that a majority of the council is composed of members who do not represent public agencies.</p>
        <p>(c) A multicounty council shall be composed of the combined membership of persons in paragraph (b).</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.413.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/423/#laws.0.5.0">1986 c 423 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/199/#laws.2.22.0">1991 c 199 art 2 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.8.25.0">1991 c 292 art 8 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/162/#laws.2.6.0">1997 c 162 art 2 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/98/#laws.1.24.0">2005 c 98 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.89.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 89</a>,96; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.414">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.414 PROCEDURES AND CRITERIA FOR DISBURSEMENT.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.414.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall establish in the plan for prevention of child abuse the criteria for distribution of trust fund money. All money shall be distributed for programs and services involving primary or secondary prevention, and no money shall be distributed for programs and services involving tertiary prevention.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.414.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Matching and other requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Trust fund money shall only be distributed to applicants that demonstrate an ability to match at least 40 percent of the amount of trust fund money requested and whose proposals meet the other criteria. The matching requirement may be met through in-kind donations. In awarding grants, the commissioner shall consider the extent to which the applicant has demonstrated a willingness and ability to:</p>
          <p>(1) continue the prevention program or service if trust fund money is eliminated or reduced; and</p>
          <p>(2) provide prevention program models and consultation to other organizations and communities.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.414.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Use of funds.</span></h2>
          <p>Priority must be given to applicants whose matching funds do not consist, in whole or in part, of state or federal funds. Any trust fund money received must not be used to compensate for a decrease in previously existing funding levels unless that decrease is attributable to a decision made by state, federal, or other entities not controlled by the applicant and the applicant demonstrates that it has made reasonable efforts to retain all previously existing funding.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.414.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Statewide or regional programs.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may fund programs that intend to serve the entire state or a region larger than the area served by any local council even if the program has not been reviewed by any local council. The commissioner may, however, solicit comments or recommendations about the applicant or the program from a local council covering any area to be served by the applicant's proposed program.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.414.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Local council as recipient of funds.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may disburse funds to a local council for community education purposes, or for administrative costs in carrying out sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.41">142A.41</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.416">142A.416</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.226#stat.144.226.3">144.226, subdivision 3</a>, if all criteria and standards are met.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.414.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Excluded programs.</span></h2>
          <p>Programs transferred to the Department of Education from the Department of Employment and Economic Development may not be included in the consolidated funding account and are ineligible for local consolidation. The commissioner may not apply for federal waivers to include these programs in funding consolidation initiatives. The programs include the following:</p>
          <p>(1) programs for the homeless under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/116L.365">116L.365</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256K.48">256K.48</a>;</p>
          <p>(2) emergency energy assistance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216C.265">216C.265</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) weatherization programs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/216C.264">216C.264</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) food shelf programs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142F.14">142F.14</a> and the emergency food assistance program; and</p>
          <p>(5) lead abatement programs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.46">142A.46</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.414.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Contracts.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner shall use state or local resources and staff if practicable but may enter into contracts with public or nonprofit private agencies to fulfill the requirements of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.41">142A.41</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.416">142A.416</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.226">144.226</a>, subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.414.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Rules.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may adopt rules to carry out sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.41">142A.41</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.416">142A.416</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.226#stat.144.226.3">144.226, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.414.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/423/#laws.0.6.0">1986 c 423 s 6</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/1/4/#laws.2.7.0">1Sp1987 c 4 art 2 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.8.25.0">1991 c 292 art 8 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/162/#laws.2.7.0">1997 c 162 art 2 s 7</a>,8; art 3 s 4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/273/#laws.0.2.0">1998 c 273 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/97/#laws.4.2.0">2005 c 97 art 4 s 2</a>,6; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/98/#laws.1.24.0">2005 c 98 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2008/0/361/#laws.3.13.0">2008 c 361 art 3 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/222/#laws.2.15.0">2014 c 222 art 2 s 15</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.90.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 90</a>-92,96; art 6 s 4; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a>,43; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/125/#laws.6.7.0">2024 c 125 art 6 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/127/#laws.51.7.0">2024 c 127 art 51 s 7</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.415">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.415 ACCEPTANCE OF FEDERAL FUNDS AND OTHER DONATIONS.</h1>
        <p>The commissioner may accept federal money and gifts, donations, and bequests for the purposes of sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.41">142A.41</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.416">142A.416</a> and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.226">144.226, subdivision </a>3. Money so received and proceeds from the sale of promotional items, minus sales promotional costs, must be deposited in the trust fund and must be made available to the commissioner.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.415.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/423/#laws.0.7.0">1986 c 423 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/358/#laws.0.114.0">1987 c 358 s 114</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.8.25.0">1991 c 292 art 8 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1997/0/162/#laws.2.9.0">1997 c 162 art 2 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/98/#laws.1.24.0">2005 c 98 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.93.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 93</a>,96; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.416">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.416 ANNUAL APPROPRIATION.</h1>
        <p>All earnings from trust fund assets, all sums received under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.415">142A.415</a>, and 60 percent of the amount collected under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/144.226#stat.144.226.3">144.226, subdivision 3</a>, are appropriated annually from the children's trust fund for the prevention of child abuse to the commissioner of children, youth, and families to carry out sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.41">142A.41</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.415">142A.415</a>.</p>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.416.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1986/0/423/#laws.0.9.0">1986 c 423 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1991/0/292/#laws.8.10.0">1991 c 292 art 8 s 10</a>,25; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1995/1/3/#laws.16.13.0">1Sp1995 c 3 art 16 s 13</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2003/0/130/#laws.0.12.0">2003 c 130 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/98/#laws.1.24.0">2005 c 98 art 1 s 24</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.94.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 94</a>,96; art 8 s 70; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.417">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.417 CHILD PROTECTION GRANTS TO ADDRESS CHILD WELFARE DISPARITIES.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.417.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Child welfare disparities grant program established.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner may award grants to eligible entities for the development, implementation, and evaluation of activities to address racial disparities and disproportionality in the child welfare system by:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) identifying and addressing structural factors that contribute to inequities in outcomes;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) identifying and implementing strategies to reduce racial disparities in treatment and outcomes;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) using cultural values, beliefs, and practices of families, communities, and tribes for case planning, service design, and decision-making processes;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) using placement and reunification strategies to maintain and support relationships and connections between parents, siblings, children, kin, significant others, and tribes; and</p>
          <p class="in">(5) supporting families in the context of their communities and tribes to safely divert them from the child welfare system, whenever possible.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.417.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">State-community partnerships; plan.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner, in partnership with the legislative task force on child protection; culturally based community organizations; the Indian Affairs Council under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/3.922">3.922</a>; the Minnesota Council on Latino Affairs under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0145">15.0145</a>; the Council for Minnesotans of African Heritage under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0145">15.0145</a>; the Council on Asian-Pacific Minnesotans under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/15.0145">15.0145</a>; the American Indian Child Welfare Advisory Council under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260.835">260.835</a>; counties; and tribal governments, shall develop and implement a comprehensive, coordinated plan to award funds under this section for the priority areas identified in subdivision 1.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.417.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Measurable outcomes.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">The commissioner, in consultation with the state-community partners listed in subdivision 2, shall establish measurable outcomes to determine the effectiveness of the grants and other activities funded under this section in reducing disparities identified in subdivision 1. The development of measurable outcomes must be completed before any funds are distributed under this section.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.417.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Process.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner, in consultation with the state-community partners listed in subdivision 2, shall develop the criteria and procedures to allocate competitive grants under this section. In developing the criteria, the commissioner shall establish an administrative cost limit for grant recipients. A county awarded a grant shall not spend more than three percent of the grant on administrative costs. When a grant is awarded, the commissioner must provide a grant recipient with information on the outcomes established according to subdivision 3.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) A grant recipient must coordinate its activities with other entities receiving funds under this section that are in the grant recipient's service area.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Grant funds must not be used to supplant any state or federal funds received for child welfare services.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.417.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Grant program criteria.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) The commissioner shall award competitive grants to eligible applicants for local or regional projects and initiatives directed at reducing disparities in the child welfare system.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commissioner may award up to 20 percent of the funds available as planning grants. Planning grants must be used to address such areas as community assessment, coordination activities, and development of community-supported strategies.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) Eligible applicants may include, but are not limited to, faith-based organizations, social service organizations, community nonprofit organizations, counties, and tribal governments. Applicants must submit proposals to the commissioner. A proposal must specify the strategies to be implemented to address one or more of the priority areas in subdivision 1 and must be targeted to achieve the outcomes established according to subdivision 3.</p>
          <p class="in">(d) The commissioner shall give priority to applicants who demonstrate that their proposed project or initiative:</p>
          <p class="in">(1) is supported by the community the applicant will serve;</p>
          <p class="in">(2) is evidence-based;</p>
          <p class="in">(3) is designed to complement other related community activities;</p>
          <p class="in">(4) utilizes strategies that positively impact priority areas;</p>
          <p class="in">(5) reflects culturally appropriate approaches; or</p>
          <p class="in">(6) will be implemented through or with community-based organizations that reflect the culture of the population to be reached.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.417.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Evaluation.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">(a) Using the outcomes established according to subdivision 3, the commissioner shall conduct a biennial evaluation of the grant program funded under this section. Grant recipients shall cooperate with the commissioner in the evaluation and shall provide the commissioner with the information needed to conduct the evaluation.</p>
          <p class="in">(b) The commissioner shall consult with the legislative task force on child protection during the evaluation process.</p>
          <p class="in">(c) The commissioner shall submit a biennial evaluation report to the task force and to the chairs and ranking minority members of the house of representatives and senate committees with jurisdiction over child protection funding. This paragraph expires January 1, 2032.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.417.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">American Indian child welfare projects.</span></h2>
          <p class="in">Of the amount appropriated for purposes of this section, the commissioner shall award $75,000 to each tribe authorized to provide tribal delivery of child welfare services under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.03#stat.142A.03.9">142A.03, subdivision 9</a>. To receive funds under this subdivision, a participating tribe is not required to apply to the commissioner for grant funds. Participating tribes are also eligible for competitive grant funds under this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.417.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.1.12.0">2015 c 71 art 1 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/77/#laws.2.87.0">2015 c 77 art 2 s 87</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2022/0/98/#laws.14.25.0">2022 c 98 art 14 s 25</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.418">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.418 PAYMENTS BY STATE.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.418.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 1.<span class="headnote">Foster care maintenance payments.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) For the purpose of foster care maintenance payments under title IV-E of the Social Security Act, United States Code, title 42, sections 670 to 676, the county or American Indian child welfare initiative Tribes under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.03#stat.142A.03.9">142A.03, subdivision 9</a>, paying the maintenance costs must be reimbursed for the costs from the federal money available for the purpose. For the purposes of determining a child's eligibility under title IV-E of the Social Security Act, the placing agency shall use AFDC requirements in effect on July 16, 1996.</p>
          <p>(b) For the purpose of foster care maintenance payments under title IV-E of the Social Security Act, United States Code, title 42, sections 670 to 676, the state is responsible for approving of child care institutions for the county paying the facility's maintenance costs to be reimbursed from the federal money available for the purpose. The facility must be licensed by the state or approved or licensed by a Tribe.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.418.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Setting foster care standard rates.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall annually establish minimum rates for foster care maintenance including supplemental difficulty of care payments for all children eligible for Northstar Care for Children under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.60">142A.60</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612">142A.612</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) All children entering foster care on or after January 1, 2015, are eligible for Northstar Care for Children under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.60">142A.60</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612">142A.612</a>. Any increase in rates shall in no case exceed three percent per annum.</p>
          <p>(c) All children in foster care on December 31, 2014, must remain in the pre-Northstar Care for Children foster care program under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.604#stat.142A.604.6">142A.604, subdivision 6</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.4411#stat.260C.4411.1">260C.4411, subdivision 1</a>. The rates for the pre-Northstar Care for Children foster care program shall remain those in effect on January 1, 2013.</p>
          <p>(d) The commissioner of children, youth, and families may promulgate rules as necessary to implement this section.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.418.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first">(<a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/1940A/cite/73">8688-16</a>) <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1937/0/438/#laws.0.14.0">1937 c 438 s 14</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1943/0/619/#laws.0.1.0">1943 c 619 s 1</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1998/0/407/#laws.9.10.0">1998 c 407 art 9 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/159/#laws.0.44.0">1999 c 159 s 44</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2000/0/260/#laws.0.97.0">2000 c 260 s 97</a>;       <div class="section" id="stat.142A.42">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.42 DIAPER DISTRIBUTION GRANT PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.42.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment; purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of children, youth, and families shall establish a diaper distribution program to award competitive grants to eligible applicants to provide diapers to underresourced families statewide.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.42.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>To be eligible for a grant under this section, an applicant must demonstrate its capacity to distribute diapers statewide by having:</p>
          <p>(1) a network of well-established partners for diaper distribution;</p>
          <p>(2) the infrastructure needed to efficiently manage diaper procurement and distribution statewide;</p>
          <p>(3) relationships with national organizations that support and enhance the work of addressing diaper need;</p>
          <p>(4) the ability to engage in building community awareness of diaper need and advocate for diaper need at local, state, and federal levels;</p>
          <p>(5) a commitment to and demonstration of working with organizations across ideological and political spectrums;</p>
          <p>(6) the ability to address diaper need for children from birth through early childhood; and</p>
          <p>(7) a commitment to working within an equity framework by ensuring access to organizations that provide culturally specific services or are located in communities with high concentrations of poverty.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.42.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Application.</span></h2>
          <p>Applicants must apply to the commissioner in a form and manner prescribed by the commissioner. Applications must be filed at the times and for the periods determined by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.42.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Eligible uses of grant money.</span></h2>
          <p>An eligible applicant that receives grant money under this section shall use the money to purchase diapers and wipes and may use up to ten percent of the money for administrative costs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.42.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Enforcement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An eligible applicant that receives grant money under this section must:</p>
          <p>(1) retain records documenting expenditure of the grant money;</p>
          <p>(2) report to the commissioner on the use of the grant money; and</p>
          <p>(3) comply with any additional requirements imposed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner may require that a report submitted under this subdivision include an independent audit.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.42.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.12.21.0">2023 c 70 art 12 s 21</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; art 8 s 70; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.13.4.0">2024 c 115 art 13 s 4</a>; art 16 s 34</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.43">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.43 GRANTS-IN-AID TO YOUTH INTERVENTION PROGRAMS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.43.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Grants.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may make grants to nonprofit agencies administering youth intervention programs in communities where the programs are or may be established.</p>
          <p>"Youth intervention program" means a nonresidential community-based program providing advocacy, education, counseling, mentoring, and referral services to youth and their families experiencing personal, familial, school, legal, or chemical problems with the goal of resolving the present problems and preventing the occurrence of the problems in the future. The intent of the youth intervention program is to provide an ongoing stable funding source to community-based early intervention programs for youth. Program design may be different for the grantees depending on youth service needs of the communities being served.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.43.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Applications.</span></h2>
          <p>Applications for a grant-in-aid shall be made by the administering agency to the commissioner.</p>
          <p>The grant-in-aid is contingent upon the agency having obtained from the community in which the youth intervention program is established local matching money equal to the amount of the grant that is sought. The matching requirement is intended to leverage the investment of state and community dollars in supporting the efforts of the grantees to provide early intervention services to youth and their families.</p>
          <p>The commissioner shall provide the application form, procedures for making application form, criteria for review of the application, and kinds of contributions in addition to cash that qualify as local matching money. No grant to any agency may exceed $75,000.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.43.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Grant allocation formula.</span></h2>
          <p>Up to five percent of the appropriations to the grants-in-aid to the youth intervention program may be used for a grant to the Minnesota Youth Intervention Programs Association for expenses in providing collaboration, program development, professional development training, technical assistance, tracking, and analyzing and reporting outcome data for the community-based grantees of the program. The Minnesota Youth Intervention Programs Association is not required to meet the match obligation under subdivision 2.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.43.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Report.</span></h2>
          <p>On or before March 31 of each year, the Minnesota Youth Intervention Programs Association shall report to the chairs and ranking minority members of the committees and divisions with jurisdiction over public safety policy and finance on the implementation, use, and administration of the grant program created under this section. The report shall include information sent by agencies administering youth intervention programs to the Minnesota Youth Intervention Programs Association and the Office of Justice Programs. At a minimum, the report must identify:</p>
          <p>(1) the grant recipients;</p>
          <p>(2) the geographic location of the grant recipients;</p>
          <p>(3) the total number of individuals served by all grant recipients, disaggregated by race, ethnicity, and gender;</p>
          <p>(4) the total number of individuals served by all grant recipients who successfully completed programming, disaggregated by age, race, ethnicity, and gender;</p>
          <p>(5) the total amount of money awarded in grants and the total amount remaining to be awarded from each appropriation;</p>
          <p>(6) the amount of money granted to each recipient;</p>
          <p>(7) grantee workplan objectives;</p>
          <p>(8) how the grant was used based on grantee quarterly narrative reports and financial reports; and</p>
          <p>(9) summarized relevant youth intervention program outcome survey data measuring the developmental assets of participants, based on Search Institute's Developmental Assets Framework.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.43.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Administrative costs.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may use up to ten percent of the biennial appropriation for grants-in-aid to the youth intervention program to pay costs incurred by the department in administering the youth intervention program.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.43.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1987/0/312/#laws.1.23.0">1987 c 312 art 1 s 23</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1996/0/408/#laws.2.2.0">1996 c 408 art 2 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/1999/0/107/#laws.0.65.0">1999 c 107 s 65</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2004/0/206/#laws.0.52.0">2004 c 206 s 52</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2005/0/136/#laws.8.1.0">2005 c 136 art 8 s 1</a>,29; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/86/#laws.3.5.0">2013 c 86 art 3 s 5</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/65/#laws.3.7.0">2015 c 65 art 3 s 7</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/52/#laws.5.22.0">2023 c 52 art 5 s 22</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/123/#laws.5.12.0">2024 c 123 art 5 s 12</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.44">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.44 GREAT START SCHOLARSHIPS PROGRAM.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.44.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Establishment; purpose.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner of children, youth, and families, in collaboration with the commissioner of education and the commissioner of human services, shall develop and, to the extent funds are available and notwithstanding federal and state laws to the contrary, implement a plan for the great start scholarships program to ensure affordable access to high-quality early care and learning for children from birth to kindergarten entry.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.44.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Development.</span></h2>
          <p>In developing the plan and implementing the program under this section, the commissioner shall:</p>
          <p>(1) identify ways to integrate the functions, administrative structures, and funding mechanisms of early care and learning programs administered by the state with the great start scholarships program;</p>
          <p>(2) consider the recommendations made by the Great Start for All Minnesota Children Task Force under Laws 2021, First Special Session chapter 7, article 14, section 18, subdivision 2;</p>
          <p>(3) create a process and timeline to transition the following families to the great start scholarships program by July 1, 2028:</p>
          <p>(i) families with at least one child receiving an early learning scholarship under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.25">142D.25</a>; and</p>
          <p>(ii) families with at least one child who is not yet in kindergarten and is receiving child care assistance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142E.04">142E.04</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142E.08">142E.08</a> for care received from a provider licensed under Minnesota Rules, chapter 9502 or 9503, or Tribally licensed, or a Head Start program that has a rating under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.13">142D.13</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) create mechanisms for members of local communities, including families and members of the early care and learning workforce, to have input in decisions regarding needs and preferences for early care and learning options;</p>
          <p>(5) develop a proposed method for funding early care and learning slots in response to local need through contracts with eligible providers that may be used to deliver services that meet quality and compensation standards with the intent to build early care and learning capacity statewide for children from birth to kindergarten entry; and</p>
          <p>(6) consider how to maximize available federal resources while maintaining access to child care assistance funding under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142E.04">142E.04</a> or <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142E.08">142E.08</a> for school-age children. The commissioner, in consultation with an appropriate state agency, may seek federal technical assistance or outside consultation as necessary to provide minimally burdensome program access to all participating families.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.44.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Program requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>The great start scholarships program must include at a minimum:</p>
          <p>(1) a method to provide financial assistance to families voluntarily participating in the program;</p>
          <p>(2) family eligibility for any qualifying family that has at least one child who is not yet in kindergarten;</p>
          <p>(3) provider eligibility for:</p>
          <p>(i) any program licensed under Minnesota Rules, chapter 9502 or 9503, or Tribally licensed, that participates in the quality rating and improvement system under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.13">142D.13</a>; and</p>
          <p>(ii) any school-based program and Head Start program that has a rating under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142D.13">142D.13</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) a unified, integrated, and simple online application process that utilizes administrative data to ease qualification and benefit determination and meet federal reporting requirements;</p>
          <p>(5) an electronic attendance tracking system that is integrated, to the extent practicable, and payments system to safeguard program integrity and streamline billing and payment processes for providers; and</p>
          <p>(6) a schedule for family contributions and provider payments that ensures that no participating family pays more than seven percent of annual income for early care and learning services for children from birth to kindergarten entry. Family contributions and provider payments may vary by family income, program quality, geography, and need for compensatory services, and may take into consideration the results of the market rate survey under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142E.02#stat.142E.02.7">142E.02, subdivision 7</a>; information from cost estimation models for providing early care and learning in the state; and cost information gathered through contracts under subdivision 2, clause (5).</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.44.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Administration; reporting requirement.</span></h2>
