If the identified parent is either unwilling or unable to adopt the child without adoption assistance, the legally responsible agency must provide documentation as prescribed by the commissioner to fulfill the requirement to make a reasonable effort to place the child without adoption assistance. If the identified parent is willing to adopt the child without adoption assistance, the parent must provide a written statement to this effect to the legally responsible agency and the statement must be maintained in the permanent adoption record of the legally responsible agency. For children under guardianship of the commissioner, the legally responsible agency shall submit a copy of this statement to the commissioner to be maintained in the permanent adoption record.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.606.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Citizenship and immigration status.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A child must be a citizen of the United States or otherwise eligible for federal public benefits according to the Personal Responsibility and Work Opportunity Reconciliation Act of 1996, as amended, in order to be eligible for the title IV-E adoption assistance program.</p>
          <p>(b) A child must be a citizen of the United States or meet the qualified alien requirements as defined in the Personal Responsibility and Work Opportunity Reconciliation Act of 1996, as amended, in order to be eligible for adoption assistance paid through funds other than title IV-E.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.606.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Background study.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A background study must be completed on each prospective adoptive parent and all other adults residing in the home. A background study must meet the requirements of United States Code, title 42, section 671(a)(20). A study completed under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/245C.33">245C.33</a> meets this requirement. If the prospective adoptive parent is a licensed child foster parent licensed under chapter 142B or by a Minnesota tribe, the background study previously completed for the purposes of child foster care licensure shall be used for the purpose of this section, provided that the background study meets all other requirements of this subdivision and the prospective adoptive parent is a licensed child foster parent at the time of the application for adoption assistance.</p>
          <p>(b) If the background study reveals:</p>
          <p>(1) a felony conviction at any time for:</p>
          <p>(i) child abuse or neglect;</p>
          <p>(ii) spousal abuse;</p>
          <p>(iii) a crime against a child, including child pornography; or</p>
          <p>(iv) a crime involving violence, including rape, sexual assault, or homicide, but not including other physical assault or battery; or</p>
          <p>(2) a felony conviction within the past five years for:</p>
          <p>(i) physical assault;</p>
          <p>(ii) battery; or</p>
          <p>(iii) a drug-related offense;</p>
          <p class="bl">the adoptive parent is prohibited from receiving adoption assistance on behalf of an otherwise eligible child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.606.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Responsibility for determining adoption assistance eligibility.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must determine eligibility for:</p>
          <p>(1) a child under the guardianship of the commissioner who would otherwise remain in foster care;</p>
          <p>(2) a child who is not under the guardianship of the commissioner who meets title IV-E eligibility defined in section 473 of the Social Security Act and no state agency has legal responsibility for placement and care of the child;</p>
          <p>(3) a Minnesota child under tribal jurisdiction who would otherwise remain in foster care; and</p>
          <p>(4) an Indian child being placed in Minnesota who meets title IV-E eligibility defined in section 473 of the Social Security Act. The agency or entity assuming responsibility for the child is responsible for the nonfederal share of the adoption assistance payment.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.606.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Exclusions.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner must not enter into an adoption assistance agreement with the following individuals:</p>
          <p>(1) a child's biological parent or stepparent;</p>
          <p>(2) a child's relative under section 260C.007, subdivision 26b or 27, with whom the child resided immediately prior to child welfare involvement unless:</p>
          <p>(i) the child was in the custody of a Minnesota county or tribal agency pursuant to an order under chapter 260C or equivalent provisions of tribal code and the agency had placement and care responsibility for permanency planning for the child; and</p>
          <p>(ii) the child is under guardianship of the commissioner of children, youth, and families according to the requirements of section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.325#stat.260C.325.1">260C.325, subdivision 1</a> or 3, or is a ward of a Minnesota tribal court after termination of parental rights, suspension of parental rights, or a finding by the tribal court that the child cannot safely return to the care of the parent;</p>
          <p>(3) an individual adopting a child who is the subject of a direct adoptive placement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/259.47">259.47</a> or the equivalent in tribal code;</p>
          <p>(4) a child's legal custodian or guardian who is now adopting the child, except for a relative custodian as defined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.602#stat.142A.602.20">142A.602, subdivision 20</a>, who is currently receiving Northstar kinship assistance benefits on behalf of the child; or</p>
          <p>(5) an individual who is adopting a child who is not a citizen or resident of the United States and was either adopted in another country or brought to the United States for the purposes of adoption.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.606.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Adoption assistance eligibility determination.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The financially responsible agency shall prepare an adoption assistance eligibility determination for review and final approval by the commissioner. When there is no financially responsible agency, the adoption assistance eligibility determination must be completed by the agency designated by the commissioner. The eligibility determination must be completed according to requirements and procedures and on forms prescribed by the commissioner. The financially responsible agency and the commissioner shall make every effort to establish a child's eligibility for title IV-E adoption assistance. Documentation from a qualified expert for the eligibility determination must be provided to the commissioner to verify that a child meets the special needs criteria in subdivision 2. A child who is determined to be eligible for adoption assistance must have an adoption assistance agreement negotiated on the child's behalf according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.608">142A.608</a>.</p>
          <p>(b) Documentation from a qualified expert of a disability is limited to evidence deemed appropriate by the commissioner and must be submitted to the commissioner with the eligibility determination. Examples of appropriate documentation include, but are not limited to, medical records, psychological assessments, educational or early childhood evaluations, court findings, and social and medical history.</p>
          <p>(c) Documentation that the child is at risk of developing physical, mental, emotional, or behavioral disabilities must be submitted according to policies and procedures prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.606.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Termination of agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) An adoption assistance agreement must terminate in any of the following circumstances:</p>
          <p>(1) the child has attained the age of 18, or up to age 21 when the child meets a condition for extension in subdivision 12;</p>
          <p>(2) the child has not attained the age of 18, but the commissioner determines the adoptive parent is no longer legally responsible for support of the child;</p>
          <p>(3) the commissioner determines the adoptive parent is no longer providing financial support to the child up to age 21;</p>
          <p>(4) the death of the child; or</p>
          <p>(5) the adoptive parent requests in writing the termination of the adoption assistance agreement.</p>
          <p>(b) An adoptive parent is considered no longer legally responsible for support of the child in any of the following circumstances:</p>
          <p>(1) parental rights to the child are legally terminated or a court accepted the parent's consent to adoption under chapter 260C;</p>
          <p>(2) permanent legal and physical custody or guardianship of the child is transferred to another individual;</p>
          <p>(3) death of the adoptive parent under subdivision 9;</p>
          <p>(4) the child enlists in the military;</p>
          <p>(5) the child gets married; or</p>
          <p>(6) the child is determined an emancipated minor through legal action.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.606.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Death of adoptive parent or adoption dissolution.</span></h2>
          <p>The adoption assistance agreement ends upon death or termination of parental rights of both adoptive parents in the case of a two-parent adoption, or the sole adoptive parent in the case of a single-parent adoption. The child's adoption assistance eligibility may be continued according to subdivision 10.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.606.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Continuing a child's title IV-E adoption assistance in a subsequent adoption.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The child maintains eligibility for title IV-E adoption assistance in a subsequent adoption if the following criteria are met:</p>
          <p>(1) the child is determined to be a child with special needs as outlined in subdivision 2; and</p>
          <p>(2) the subsequent adoptive parent resides in Minnesota.</p>
          <p>(b) If a child had a title IV-E adoption assistance agreement in effect prior to the death of the adoptive parent or dissolution of the adoption, and the subsequent adoptive parent resides outside of Minnesota, the commissioner is not responsible for determining whether the child meets the definition of special needs, entering into the adoption assistance agreement, and making any adoption assistance payments outlined in the new agreement unless a state agency in Minnesota has responsibility for placement and care of the child at the time of the subsequent adoption. If there is no state agency in Minnesota that has responsibility for placement and care of the child at the time of the subsequent adoption, the public child welfare agency in the subsequent adoptive parent's residence is responsible for determining whether the child meets the definition of special needs and entering into the adoption assistance agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.606.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Assigning a child's adoption assistance to a court-appointed guardian or custodian.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) State-funded adoption assistance may be continued with the written consent of the commissioner to an individual who is a guardian appointed by a court for the child upon the death of both the adoptive parents in the case of a two-parent adoption, or the sole adoptive parent in the case of a single-parent adoption, unless the child is under the custody of a state agency.</p>
          <p>(b) Temporary assignment of adoption assistance may be approved by the commissioner for a maximum of six consecutive months from the death of the adoptive parent or parents under subdivision 9 and must adhere to the requirements and procedures prescribed by the commissioner. If, within six months, the child has not been adopted by a person agreed upon by the commissioner, or a court has not appointed a permanent legal guardian under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.325">260C.325</a>, <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/524.5-313">524.5-313</a>, or similar law of another jurisdiction, the adoption assistance must terminate.</p>
          <p>(c) Upon assignment of payments under this subdivision, assistance must be from funds other than title IV-E.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.606.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Extension of adoption assistance agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Under certain limited circumstances a child may qualify for extension of the adoption assistance agreement beyond the date the child attains age 18, up to the date the child attains the age of 21.</p>
          <p>(b) A request for extension of the adoption assistance agreement must be completed in writing and submitted, including all supporting documentation, by the adoptive parent to the commissioner at least 60 calendar days prior to the date that the current agreement will terminate.</p>
          <p>(c) A signed amendment to the current adoption assistance agreement must be fully executed between the adoptive parent and the commissioner at least ten business days prior to the termination of the current agreement. The request for extension and the fully executed amendment must be made according to the requirements and procedures prescribed by the commissioner, including documentation of eligibility, on forms prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(d) If an agency is certifying a child for adoption assistance and the child will attain the age of 18 within 60 calendar days of submission, the request for extension must be completed in writing and submitted, including all supporting documentation, with the adoption assistance application.</p>
          <p>(e) A child who has attained the age of 16 prior to the finalization of the child's adoption is eligible for extension of the adoption assistance agreement up to the date the child attains age 21 if the child is:</p>
          <p>(1) dependent on the adoptive parent for care and financial support; and</p>
          <p>(2)(i) completing a secondary education program or a program leading to an equivalent credential;</p>
          <p>(ii) enrolled in an institution that provides postsecondary or vocational education;</p>
          <p>(iii) participating in a program or activity designed to promote or remove barriers to employment;</p>
          <p>(iv) employed for at least 80 hours per month; or</p>
          <p>(v) incapable of doing any of the activities described in items (i) to (iv) due to a medical condition where incapability is supported by documentation from an expert according to the requirements and procedures prescribed by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(f) A child who has not attained the age of 16 prior to finalization of the child's adoption is eligible for extension of the adoption assistance agreement up to the date the child attains the age of 21 if the child is:</p>
          <p>(1) dependent on the adoptive parent for care and financial support; and</p>
          <p>(2)(i) enrolled in a secondary education program or a program leading to the equivalent; or</p>
          <p>(ii) possesses a physical or mental disability that impairs the capacity for independent living and warrants continuation of financial assistance as determined by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.606.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Beginning adoption assistance under Northstar Care for Children.</span></h2>
          <p>Effective November 27, 2014, a child who meets the eligibility criteria for adoption assistance in subdivision 1 may have an adoption assistance agreement negotiated on the child's behalf according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.608">142A.608</a>, and the effective date of the agreement must be January 1, 2015, or the date of the court order finalizing the adoption, whichever is later. Except as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609#stat.142A.609.1">142A.609, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c), the maximum rate schedule for the agreement must be determined according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609">142A.609</a> based on the age of the child on the date that the prospective adoptive parent or parents sign the agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.606.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Transition to adoption assistance under Northstar Care for Children.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner may offer adoption assistance agreements under this chapter to a child with an adoption assistance agreement under chapter 259A executed on the child's behalf on or before November 26, 2014, according to the priorities outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612#stat.142A.612.7">142A.612, subdivision 7</a>, paragraph (b). To facilitate transition into the Northstar Care for Children adoption assistance program, the commissioner has the authority to waive any Northstar Care for Children adoption assistance eligibility requirements for a child with an adoption assistance agreement under chapter 259A executed on the child's behalf on or before November 26, 2014. Agreements negotiated under this subdivision must be in accordance with the process in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612#stat.142A.612.7">142A.612, subdivision 7</a>. The maximum rate used in the negotiation process for an agreement under this subdivision must be as outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612#stat.142A.612.7">142A.612, subdivision 7</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.606.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.17.10.0">2013 c 108 art 17 s 10</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.25.20.0">2014 c 312 art 25 s 20</a>,21; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/78/#laws.1.9.0">2015 c 78 art 1 s 9</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2019/0/50/#laws.1.80.0">2019 c 50 art 1 s 80</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.9.4.0">2021 c 30 art 9 s 4</a>,5; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; art 2 s 74; art 8 s 70; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a>,41</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.607">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.607 ASSESSMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Assessment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Each child eligible under sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.604">142A.604</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.606">142A.606</a>, must be assessed to determine the benefits the child may receive under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609">142A.609</a>, in accordance with the assessment tool, process, and requirements specified in subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(b) If an agency applies the emergency foster care rate for initial placement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609">142A.609</a>, the agency may wait up to 30 days to complete the initial assessment.</p>
          <p>(c) Unless otherwise specified in paragraph (d), a child must be assessed at the basic level, level B, or one of ten supplemental difficulty of care levels, levels C to L.</p>
          <p>(d) An assessment must not be completed for:</p>
          <p>(1) a child eligible for Northstar adoption assistance under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.606">142A.606</a> who is determined to be an at-risk child. A child under this clause must be assigned level A under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609#stat.142A.609.1">142A.609, subdivision 1</a>; and</p>
          <p>(2) a child transitioning into Northstar Care for Children under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612">142A.612</a>, subdivision 7, unless the commissioner determines an assessment is appropriate.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Establishment of assessment tool, process, and requirements.</span></h2>
          <p>Consistent with sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.60">142A.60</a> to <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612">142A.612</a>, the commissioner shall establish an assessment tool to determine the basic and supplemental difficulty of care, and shall establish the process to be followed and other requirements, including appropriate documentation, when conducting the initial assessment of a child entering Northstar Care for Children or when the special assessment and reassessments may be needed for children continuing in the program. The assessment tool must take into consideration the strengths and needs of the child and the extra parenting provided by the caregiver to meet the child's needs.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Minnesota assessment of parenting for children and youth (MAPCY) revision.</span></h2>
          <p>The commissioner, in consultation with representatives from communities of color, including but not limited to advisory councils and ombudspersons, shall review and revise the MAPCY tool and incorporate changes that take into consideration different cultures and the diverse needs of communities of color.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Child care allowance portion of assessment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The assessment tool established under subdivision 2 must include consideration of the caregiver's need for child care under this subdivision, with greater consideration for children of younger ages.</p>
          <p>(b) The child's assessment must include consideration of the caregiver's need for child care if the following criteria are met:</p>
          <p>(1) the child is under age 13;</p>
          <p>(2) all available adult caregivers are employed or attending educational or vocational training programs; and</p>
          <p>(3) the caregiver does not receive child care assistance for the child under chapter 142E.</p>
          <p>(c) For children younger than seven years of age, the level determined by the non-child care portions of the assessment must be adjusted based on the average number of hours child care is needed each week due to employment or attending a training or educational program as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) fewer than ten hours or if the caregiver is participating in the child care assistance program under chapter 119B, no adjustment;</p>
          <p>(2) ten to 19 hours or if needed during school summer vacation or equivalent only, increase one level;</p>
          <p>(3) 20 to 29 hours, increase two levels;</p>
          <p>(4) 30 to 39 hours, increase three levels; and</p>
          <p>(5) 40 or more hours, increase four levels.</p>
          <p>(d) For children at least seven years of age but younger than 13, the level determined by the non-child care portions of the assessment must be adjusted based on the average number of hours child care is needed each week due to employment or attending a training or educational program as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) fewer than 20 hours, needed during school summer vacation or equivalent only, or if the caregiver is participating in the child care assistance program under chapter 142E, no adjustment;</p>
          <p>(2) 20 to 39 hours, increase one level; and</p>
          <p>(3) 40 or more hours, increase two levels.</p>
          <p>(e) When the child attains the age of seven, the child care allowance must be reduced by reducing the level to that available under paragraph (d). For children in foster care, benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609">142A.609</a> must be automatically reduced when the child turns seven. For children who receive Northstar kinship assistance or adoption assistance, agreements must include similar provisions to ensure that the benefit provided to these children does not exceed the benefit provided to children in foster care.</p>
          <p>(f) When the child attains the age of 13, the child care allowance must be eliminated by reducing the level to that available prior to any consideration of the caregiver's need for child care. For children in foster care, benefits under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609">142A.609</a> must be automatically reduced when the child attains the age of 13. For children who receive Northstar kinship assistance or adoption assistance, agreements must include similar provisions to ensure that the benefit provided to these children does not exceed the benefit provided to children in foster care.</p>
          <p>(g) The child care allowance under this subdivision is not available to caregivers who receive the child care assistance under chapter 142E. A caregiver receiving a child care allowance under this subdivision must notify the commissioner if the caregiver subsequently receives the child care assistance program under chapter 142E, and the level must be reduced to that available prior to any consideration of the caregiver's need for child care.</p>
          <p>(h) In establishing the assessment tool under subdivision 2, the commissioner must design the tool so that the levels applicable to the non-child care portions of the assessment at a given age accommodate the requirements of this subdivision.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Extraordinary levels.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The assessment tool established under subdivision 2 must provide a mechanism through which up to five levels can be added to the supplemental difficulty of care for a particular child under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609">142A.609</a>, subdivision 4. In establishing the assessment tool, the commissioner must design the tool so that the levels applicable to the portions of the assessment other than the extraordinary levels can accommodate the requirements of this subdivision.</p>
          <p>(b) These extraordinary levels are available when all of the following circumstances apply:</p>
          <p>(1) the child has extraordinary needs as determined by the assessment tool provided for under subdivision 2, and the child meets other requirements established by the commissioner, such as a minimum score on the assessment tool;</p>
          <p>(2) the child's extraordinary needs require extraordinary care and intense supervision that is provided by the child's caregiver as part of the parental duties as described in the supplemental difficulty of care rate, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.602#stat.142A.602.22">142A.602, subdivision 22</a>. This extraordinary care provided by the caregiver is required so that the child can be safely cared for in the home and community, and prevents residential placement;</p>
          <p>(3) the child is physically living in a foster family setting, as defined in Minnesota Rules, part <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/rules/2960.3010">2960.3010</a>, subpart 23, or physically living in the home with the adoptive parent or relative custodian; and</p>
          <p>(4) the child is receiving the services for which the child is eligible through medical assistance programs or other programs that provide necessary services for children with disabilities or other medical and behavioral conditions to live with the child's family, but the agency with caregiver's input has identified a specific support gap that cannot be met through home and community support waivers or other programs that are designed to provide support for children with special needs.</p>
          <p>(c) The agency completing an assessment, under subdivision 2, that suggests an extraordinary level must document as part of the assessment, the following:</p>
          <p>(1) the assessment tool that determined that the child's needs or disabilities require extraordinary care and intense supervision;</p>
          <p>(2) a summary of the extraordinary care and intense supervision that is provided by the caregiver as part of the parental duties as described in the supplemental difficulty of care rate, section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.602#stat.142A.602.22">142A.602, subdivision 22</a>;</p>
          <p>(3) confirmation that the child is currently physically residing in the home with the foster parent, adoptive parent, or relative custodian;</p>
          <p>(4) the efforts of the agency, caregiver, parents, and others to request support services in the home and community that would ease the degree of parental duties provided by the caregiver for the care and supervision of the child. This would include documentation of the services provided for the child's needs or disabilities, and the services that were denied or not available from the local social service agency, community agency, the local school district, local public health department, the parent, or child's medical insurance provider;</p>
          <p>(5) the specific support gap identified that places the child's safety and well-being at risk in the home or community and is necessary to prevent residential placement; and</p>
          <p>(6) the extraordinary care and intense supervision provided by the foster, adoptive, or guardianship caregivers to maintain the child safely in the child's home and prevent residential placement that cannot be supported by medical assistance or other programs that provide services, necessary care for children with disabilities, or other medical or behavioral conditions in the home or community.</p>
          <p>(d) An agency completing an assessment under subdivision 2 that suggests an extraordinary level is appropriate must forward the assessment and required documentation to the commissioner. If the commissioner approves, the extraordinary levels must be retroactive to the date the assessment was forwarded.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Timing of initial assessment.</span></h2>
          <p>For a child entering Northstar Care for Children under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.604">142A.604</a>, the initial assessment must be completed within 30 days after the child is placed in foster care.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Completion of initial assessment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The assessment must be completed in consultation with the child's caregiver. Face-to-face contact with the caregiver is not required to complete the assessment.</p>
          <p>(b) Initial assessments are completed for foster children, eligible under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.604">142A.604</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) The initial assessment must be completed by the financially responsible agency, in consultation with the legally responsible agency if different, within 30 days of the child's placement in foster care.</p>
          <p>(d) If the foster parent is unable or unwilling to cooperate with the assessment process, the child shall be assigned the basic level, level B under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609#stat.142A.609.3">142A.609, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) Notice to the foster parent shall be provided as specified in subdivision 13.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Timing of special assessment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A special assessment is required as part of the negotiation of the Northstar kinship assistance agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.605">142A.605</a> if:</p>
          <p>(1) the child was not placed in foster care with the prospective relative custodian or custodians prior to the negotiation of the Northstar kinship assistance agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.608">142A.608</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) any requirement for reassessment under subdivision 10 is met.</p>
          <p>(b) A special assessment is required as part of the negotiation of the adoption assistance agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.606">142A.606</a> if:</p>
          <p>(1) the child was not placed in foster care with the prospective adoptive parent or parents prior to the negotiation of the adoption assistance agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.608">142A.608</a>; or</p>
          <p>(2) any requirement for reassessment under subdivision 10 is met.</p>
          <p>(c) A special assessment is required when a child transitions from a pre-Northstar Care for Children program into Northstar Care for Children if the commissioner determines that a special assessment is appropriate instead of assigning the transition child to a level under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612">142A.612</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) The special assessment must be completed prior to the establishment of a Northstar kinship assistance or adoption assistance agreement on behalf of the child.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Completing the special assessment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The special assessment must be completed in consultation with the child's caregiver. Face-to-face contact with the caregiver is not required to complete the special assessment.</p>
          <p>(b) If a new special assessment is required prior to the effective date of the Northstar kinship assistance agreement, it must be completed by the financially responsible agency, in consultation with the legally responsible agency if different. If the prospective relative custodian is unable or unwilling to cooperate with the special assessment process, the child shall be assigned the basic level, level B under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609#stat.142A.609.3">142A.609, subdivision 3</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) If a special assessment is required prior to the effective date of the adoption assistance agreement, it must be completed by the financially responsible agency, in consultation with the legally responsible agency if different. If there is no financially responsible agency, the special assessment must be completed by the agency designated by the commissioner. If the prospective adoptive parent is unable or unwilling to cooperate with the special assessment process, the child must be assigned the basic level, level B under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609#stat.142A.609.3">142A.609, subdivision 3</a>, unless the child is known to be an at-risk child, in which case, the child shall be assigned level A under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609#stat.142A.609.1">142A.609, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) Notice to the prospective relative custodians or prospective adoptive parents must be provided as specified in subdivision 14.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Timing of and requests for reassessments.</span></h2>
          <p>Reassessments for an eligible child must be completed within 30 days of any of the following events:</p>
          <p>(1) for a child in continuous foster care, when six months have elapsed since the initial assessment, and annually thereafter;</p>
          <p>(2) for a child in continuous foster care, change of placement location;</p>
          <p>(3) for a child in foster care, at the request of the financially responsible agency or legally responsible agency;</p>
          <p>(4) at the request of the commissioner; or</p>
          <p>(5) at the request of the caregiver under subdivision 11.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.11">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 11.<span class="headnote">Caregiver requests for reassessments.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A caregiver may initiate a reassessment request for an eligible child in writing to the financially responsible agency or, if there is no financially responsible agency, the agency designated by the commissioner. The written request must include the reason for the request and the name, address, and contact information of the caregivers. The caregiver may request a reassessment if at least six months have elapsed since any previous assessment or reassessment. For an eligible foster child, a foster parent may request reassessment in less than six months with written documentation that there have been significant changes in the child's needs that necessitate an earlier reassessment.</p>
          <p>(b) A caregiver may request a reassessment of an at-risk child for whom an adoption assistance agreement has been executed if the caregiver has satisfied the commissioner with written documentation from a qualified expert that the potential disability upon which eligibility for the agreement was based has manifested itself, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.608#stat.142A.608.4">142A.608, subdivision 4</a>, paragraph (b).</p>
          <p>(c) If the reassessment cannot be completed within 30 days of the caregiver's request, the agency responsible for reassessment must notify the caregiver of the reason for the delay and a reasonable estimate of when the reassessment can be completed.</p>
          <p>(d) Notwithstanding any provision to the contrary in paragraph (a) or subdivision 10, when a Northstar kinship assistance agreement or adoption assistance agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.608">142A.608</a> has been signed by all parties, no reassessment may be requested or conducted until the court finalizes the transfer of permanent legal and physical custody or finalizes the adoption.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.12">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 12.<span class="headnote">Completion of reassessment.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The reassessment must be completed in consultation with the child's caregiver. Face-to-face contact with the caregiver is not required to complete the reassessment.</p>
          <p>(b) For foster children eligible under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.604">142A.604</a>, reassessments must be completed by the financially responsible agency, in consultation with the legally responsible agency if different.</p>
          <p>(c) If reassessment is required after the effective date of the Northstar kinship assistance agreement, the reassessment must be completed by the financially responsible agency.</p>
          <p>(d) If a reassessment is required after the effective date of the adoption assistance agreement, it must be completed by the financially responsible agency or, if there is no financially responsible agency, the agency designated by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(e) If the child's caregiver is unable or unwilling to cooperate with the reassessment, the child must be assessed at level B under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609#stat.142A.609.3">142A.609, subdivision 3</a>, unless the child has a Northstar adoption assistance agreement and is known to be an at-risk child, in which case the child must be assessed at level A under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609#stat.142A.609.1">142A.609, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.13">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 13.<span class="headnote">Approval of initial assessments, special assessments, and reassessments.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Any agency completing initial assessments, special assessments, or reassessments must designate one or more supervisors or other staff to examine and approve assessments completed by others in the agency under subdivision 2. The person approving an assessment must not be the case manager or staff member completing that assessment.</p>
          <p>(b) In cases where a special assessment or reassessment for Northstar kinship assistance and adoption assistance is required under subdivision 9 or 12, the commissioner shall review and approve the assessment as part of the eligibility determination process outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.605#stat.142A.605.7">142A.605, subdivision 7</a>, for Northstar kinship assistance, or section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.606">142A.606</a>, subdivision 7, for adoption assistance. The assessment determines the maximum of the negotiated agreement amount under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.608">142A.608</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) The effective date of the new rate is determined as follows:</p>
          <p>(1) for initial assessments of children in foster care, the new rate is effective based on the emergency foster care rate for initial placement pursuant to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609">142A.609, subdivision </a>6;</p>
          <p>(2) for special assessments, the new rate is effective on the date of the finalized adoption decree or the date of the court order that transfers permanent legal and physical custody to a relative;</p>
          <p>(3) for postpermanency reassessments, the new rate is effective on the date that the commissioner signs the amendment to the Northstar Adoption Assistance or Northstar Kinship Assistance benefit agreement.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.14">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 14.<span class="headnote">Notice for caregiver.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The agency as defined in subdivision 7 or 12 that is responsible for completing the initial assessment or reassessment must provide the child's caregiver with written notice of the initial assessment or reassessment.</p>
          <p>(b) Initial assessment notices must be sent within 15 days of completion of the initial assessment and must minimally include the following:</p>
          <p>(1) a summary of the child's completed individual assessment used to determine the initial rating;</p>
          <p>(2) statement of rating and benefit level;</p>
          <p>(3) statement of the circumstances under which the agency must reassess the child;</p>
          <p>(4) procedure to seek reassessment;</p>
          <p>(5) notice that the caregiver has the right to a fair hearing review of the assessment and how to request a fair hearing, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.045#stat.256.045.3">256.045, subdivision 3</a>; and</p>
          <p>(6) the name, telephone number, and email, if available, of a contact person at the agency completing the assessment.</p>
          <p>(c) Reassessment notices must be sent within 15 days after the completion of the reassessment and must minimally include the following:</p>
          <p>(1) a summary of the child's individual assessment used to determine the new rating;</p>
          <p>(2) any change in rating and its effective date;</p>
          <p>(3) procedure to seek reassessment;</p>
          <p>(4) notice that if a change in rating results in a reduction of benefits, the caregiver has the right to a fair hearing review of the assessment and how to request a fair hearing consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256.045#stat.256.045.3">256.045, subdivision 3</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) notice that a caregiver who requests a fair hearing of the reassessed rating within ten days may continue at the current rate pending the hearing, but the agency may recover any overpayment; and</p>
          <p>(6) name, telephone number, and email, if available, of a contact person at the agency completing the reassessment.</p>
          <p>(d) Notice is not required for special assessments since the notice is part of the Northstar kinship assistance or adoption assistance negotiated agreement completed according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.608">142A.608</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.607.15">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 15.<span class="headnote">Assessment tool determines rate of benefits.</span></h2>
          <p>The assessment tool established by the commissioner in subdivision 2 determines the monthly benefit level for children in foster care. The monthly payment for Northstar kinship assistance or adoption assistance may be negotiated up to the monthly benefit level under foster care for those children eligible for a payment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609#stat.142A.609.1">142A.609, subdivision 1</a>.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.607.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.17.11.0">2013 c 108 art 17 s 11</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/275/#laws.1.74.0">2014 c 275 art 1 s 74</a>-79; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.25.22.0">2014 c 312 art 25 s 22</a>,23,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.1.49.0">2015 c 71 art 1 s 49</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2018/0/188/#laws.0.2.0">2018 c 188 s 2</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2020/1/2/#laws.5.63.0">1Sp2020 c 2 art 5 s 63</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.9.6.0">2021 c 30 art 9 s 6</a>-10; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2023/0/70/#laws.14.4.0">2023 c 70 art 14 s 4</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; art 4 s 26; art 5 s 7; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a>,42; art 18 s 16</p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.608">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.608 AGREEMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.608.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Agreement; Northstar kinship assistance; adoption assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) In order to receive Northstar kinship assistance or adoption assistance benefits on behalf of an eligible child, a written, binding agreement between the caregiver or caregivers, the financially responsible agency, or, if there is no financially responsible agency, the agency designated by the commissioner, and the commissioner must be established prior to finalization of the adoption or a transfer of permanent legal and physical custody. The agreement must be negotiated with the caregiver or caregivers under subdivision 3 and renegotiated under subdivision 4, if applicable.</p>
          <p>(b) The agreement must be on a form approved by the commissioner and must specify the following:</p>
          <p>(1) duration of the agreement;</p>
          <p>(2) the nature and amount of any payment, services, and assistance to be provided under such agreement;</p>
          <p>(3) the child's eligibility for Medicaid services;</p>
          <p>(4) the terms of the payment, including any child care portion as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607#stat.142A.607.4">142A.607, subdivision 4</a>;</p>
          <p>(5) eligibility for reimbursement of nonrecurring expenses associated with adopting or obtaining permanent legal and physical custody of the child, to the extent that the total cost does not exceed $2,000 per child pursuant to subdivision 2;</p>
          <p>(6) that the agreement must remain in effect regardless of the state of which the adoptive parents or relative custodians are residents at any given time;</p>
          <p>(7) provisions for modification of the terms of the agreement, including renegotiation of the agreement;</p>
          <p>(8) the effective date of the agreement; and</p>
          <p>(9) the successor relative custodian or custodians for Northstar kinship assistance, when applicable. The successor relative custodian or custodians may be added or changed by mutual agreement under subdivision 4.</p>
          <p>(c) The caregivers, the commissioner, and the financially responsible agency, or, if there is no financially responsible agency, the agency designated by the commissioner, must sign the agreement. A copy of the signed agreement must be given to each party. Once signed by all parties, the commissioner shall maintain the official record of the agreement.</p>
          <p>(d) The effective date of the Northstar kinship assistance agreement must be the date of the court order that transfers permanent legal and physical custody to the relative. The effective date of the adoption assistance agreement is the date of the finalized adoption decree.</p>
          <p>(e) Termination or disruption of the preadoptive placement or the foster care placement prior to assignment of custody makes the agreement with that caregiver void.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.608.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Reimbursement of nonrecurring expenses.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner of children, youth, and families must reimburse a relative custodian with a fully executed Northstar kinship assistance benefit agreement for costs that the relative custodian incurs while seeking permanent legal and physical custody of a child who is the subject of a Northstar kinship assistance benefit agreement. The commissioner must reimburse a relative custodian for expenses that are reasonable and necessary that the relative incurs during the transfer of permanent legal and physical custody of a child to the relative custodian, subject to a maximum of $2,000. To be eligible for reimbursement, the expenses must directly relate to the legal transfer of permanent legal and physical custody of the child to the relative custodian, must not have been incurred by the relative custodian in violation of state or federal law, and must not have been reimbursed from other sources or funds. The relative custodian must submit reimbursement requests to the commissioner within 21 months of the date of the child's finalized transfer of permanent legal and physical custody, and the relative custodian must follow all requirements and procedures that the commissioner prescribes.</p>
          <p>(b) The commissioner of children, youth, and families must reimburse an adoptive parent for costs that the adoptive parent incurs in an adoption of a child with special needs according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.606#stat.142A.606.2">142A.606, subdivision 2</a>. The commissioner must reimburse an adoptive parent for expenses that are reasonable and necessary for the adoption of the child to occur, subject to a maximum of $2,000. To be eligible for reimbursement, the expenses must directly relate to the legal adoption of the child, must not have been incurred by the adoptive parent in violation of state or federal law, and must not have been reimbursed from other sources or funds.</p>
          <p>(1) Children who have special needs but who are not citizens or residents of the United States and were either adopted in another country or brought to this country for the purposes of adoption are categorically ineligible for the reimbursement program in this section, except when the child meets the eligibility criteria in this section after the dissolution of the child's international adoption.</p>
          <p>(2) An adoptive parent, in consultation with the responsible child-placing agency, may request reimbursement of nonrecurring adoption expenses by submitting a complete application to the commissioner that follows the commissioner's requirements and procedures on forms that the commissioner prescribes.</p>
          <p>(3) The commissioner must determine a child's eligibility for adoption expense reimbursement under title IV-E of the Social Security Act, United States Code, title 42, sections 670 to 679c. If the commissioner determines that a child is eligible, the commissioner of children, youth, and families must fully execute the agreement for nonrecurring adoption expense reimbursement by signing the agreement. For a child to be eligible, the commissioner must have fully executed the agreement for nonrecurring adoption expense reimbursement prior to finalizing a child's adoption.</p>
          <p>(4) An adoptive parent who has a fully executed Northstar adoption assistance agreement is not required to submit a separate application for reimbursement of nonrecurring adoption expenses for the child who is the subject of the Northstar adoption assistance agreement.</p>
          <p>(5) If the commissioner has determined the child to be eligible, the adoptive parent must submit reimbursement requests to the commissioner within 21 months of the date of the child's adoption decree, and the adoptive parent must follow requirements and procedures that the commissioner prescribes.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.608.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Negotiation of agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) When a child is determined to be eligible for Northstar kinship assistance or adoption assistance, the financially responsible agency, or, if there is no financially responsible agency, the agency designated by the commissioner, must negotiate with the caregiver to develop an agreement under subdivision 1. If and when the caregiver and agency reach concurrence as to the terms of the agreement, both parties shall sign the agreement. The agency must submit the agreement, along with the eligibility determination outlined in sections <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.605#stat.142A.605.7">142A.605, subdivision 7</a>, and <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.606#stat.142A.606.7">142A.606, subdivision 7</a>, to the commissioner for final review, approval, and signature according to subdivision 1.</p>
          <p>(b) A monthly payment is provided as part of the adoption assistance or Northstar kinship assistance agreement to support the care of children unless the child is eligible for adoption assistance and determined to be an at-risk child, in which case no payment will be made unless and until the caregiver obtains written documentation from a qualified expert that the potential disability upon which eligibility for the agreement was based has manifested itself.</p>
          <p>(1) The amount of the payment made on behalf of a child eligible for Northstar kinship assistance or adoption assistance is determined through agreement between the prospective relative custodian or the adoptive parent and the financially responsible agency, or, if there is no financially responsible agency, the agency designated by the commissioner, using the assessment tool established by the commissioner in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607#stat.142A.607.2">142A.607, subdivision 2</a>, and the associated benefit and payments outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609">142A.609</a>. Except as provided under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607#stat.142A.607.1">142A.607, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (c), the assessment tool establishes the monthly benefit level for a child under foster care. The monthly payment under a Northstar kinship assistance agreement or adoption assistance agreement may be negotiated up to the monthly benefit level under foster care. In no case may the amount of the payment under a Northstar kinship assistance agreement or adoption assistance agreement exceed the foster care maintenance payment which would have been paid during the month if the child with respect to whom the Northstar kinship assistance or adoption assistance payment is made had been in a foster family home in the state.</p>
          <p>(2) The rate schedule for the agreement is determined based on the age of the child on the date that the prospective adoptive parent or parents or relative custodian or custodians sign the agreement.</p>
          <p>(3) The income of the relative custodian or custodians or adoptive parent or parents must not be taken into consideration when determining eligibility for Northstar kinship assistance or adoption assistance or the amount of the payments under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609">142A.609</a>.</p>
          <p>(4) With the concurrence of the relative custodian or adoptive parent, the amount of the payment may be adjusted periodically using the assessment tool established by the commissioner in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607#stat.142A.607.2">142A.607, subdivision 2</a>, and the agreement renegotiated under subdivision 4 when there is a change in the child's needs or the family's circumstances.</p>
          <p>(5) An adoptive parent of an at-risk child with an adoption assistance agreement may request a reassessment of the child under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607#stat.142A.607.11">142A.607, subdivision 11</a>, and renegotiation of the adoption assistance agreement under subdivision 4 to include a monthly payment, if the caregiver has written documentation from a qualified expert that the potential disability upon which eligibility for the agreement was based has manifested itself. Documentation of the disability must be limited to evidence deemed appropriate by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) For Northstar kinship assistance agreements:</p>
          <p>(1) the initial amount of the monthly Northstar kinship assistance payment must be equivalent to the foster care rate in effect at the time that the agreement is signed, or a lesser negotiated amount if agreed to by the prospective relative custodian and specified in that agreement, unless the Northstar kinship assistance agreement is entered into when a child is under the age of six; and</p>
          <p>(2) the amount of the monthly payment for a Northstar kinship assistance agreement for a child who is under the age of six must be as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609#stat.142A.609.5">142A.609, subdivision 5</a>.</p>
          <p>(d) For adoption assistance agreements:</p>
          <p>(1) for a child in foster care with the prospective adoptive parent immediately prior to adoptive placement, the initial amount of the monthly adoption assistance payment must be equivalent to the foster care rate in effect at the time that the agreement is signed, or a lesser negotiated amount if agreed to by the prospective adoptive parents and specified in that agreement, unless the child is identified as at-risk or the adoption assistance agreement is entered into when a child is under the age of six;</p>
          <p>(2) for an at-risk child who must be assigned level A as outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609">142A.609</a>, no payment will be made unless and until the potential disability manifests itself, as documented by an appropriate professional, and the commissioner authorizes commencement of payment by modifying the agreement accordingly;</p>
          <p>(3) the amount of the monthly payment for an adoption assistance agreement for a child under the age of six, other than an at-risk child, must be as specified in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609#stat.142A.609.5">142A.609, subdivision 5</a>;</p>
          <p>(4) for a child who is in the Northstar kinship assistance program immediately prior to adoptive placement, the initial amount of the adoption assistance payment must be equivalent to the Northstar kinship assistance payment in effect at the time that the adoption assistance agreement is signed or a lesser amount if agreed to by the prospective adoptive parent and specified in that agreement, unless the child is identified as an at-risk child; and</p>
          <p>(5) for a child who is not in foster care placement or the Northstar kinship assistance program immediately prior to adoptive placement or negotiation of the adoption assistance agreement, the initial amount of the adoption assistance agreement must be determined using the assessment tool and process in this section and the corresponding payment amount outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609">142A.609</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.608.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Renegotiation of agreement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A relative custodian or adoptive parent of a child with a Northstar kinship assistance or adoption assistance agreement may request renegotiation of the agreement when there is a change in the needs of the child or in the family's circumstances. When a relative custodian or adoptive parent requests renegotiation of the agreement, a reassessment of the child must be completed consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607">142A.607, subdivisions 11</a> and 12. If the reassessment indicates that the child's level has changed, the financially responsible agency or, if there is no financially responsible agency, the agency designated by the commissioner or the commissioner's designee, and the caregiver must renegotiate the agreement to include a payment with the level determined through the reassessment process. The agreement must not be renegotiated unless the commissioner, the financially responsible agency, and the caregiver mutually agree to the changes. The effective date of any renegotiated agreement must be determined by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(b) An adoptive parent of an at-risk child with an adoption assistance agreement may request renegotiation of the agreement to include a monthly payment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.609">142A.609 </a>if the caregiver has written documentation from a qualified expert that the potential disability upon which eligibility for the agreement was based has manifested itself. Documentation of the disability must be limited to evidence deemed appropriate by the commissioner. Prior to renegotiating the agreement, a reassessment of the child must be conducted as outlined in section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607#stat.142A.607.11">142A.607, subdivision 11</a>. The reassessment must be used to renegotiate the agreement to include an appropriate monthly payment. The agreement must not be renegotiated unless the commissioner, the financially responsible agency, and the caregiver mutually agree to the changes. The effective date of any renegotiated agreement must be determined by the commissioner.</p>
        </div>
      </div>
      <div class="history" id="stat.142A.608.history
			">
        <h2>History: </h2>
        <p class="first"><a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2013/0/108/#laws.17.12.0">2013 c 108 art 17 s 12</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/275/#laws.1.80.0">2014 c 275 art 1 s 80</a>,81; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2014/0/312/#laws.25.24.0">2014 c 312 art 25 s 24</a>,25,34; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2015/0/71/#laws.1.50.0">2015 c 71 art 1 s 50</a>; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/0/30/#laws.9.11.0">2021 c 30 art 9 s 11</a>,12; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2021/1/7/#laws.9.1.0">1Sp2021 c 7 art 9 s 1</a>,2; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/80/#laws.1.96.0">2024 c 80 art 1 s 96</a>; art 8 s 70; <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/laws/2024/0/115/#laws.16.34.0">2024 c 115 art 16 s 34</a></p>
      </div>
      <div class="section" id="stat.142A.609">
        <h1 class="shn">142A.609 BENEFITS AND PAYMENTS.</h1>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.609.1">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subdivision 1.<span class="headnote">Benefits.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) There are three benefits under Northstar Care for Children: medical assistance, basic payment, and supplemental difficulty of care payment.</p>
          <p>(b) A child is eligible for medical assistance under subdivision 2.</p>
          <p>(c) A child is eligible for the basic payment under subdivision 3, except for a child assigned level A under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607#stat.142A.607.1">142A.607, subdivision 1</a>, because the child is determined to be an at-risk child receiving adoption assistance.</p>
          <p>(d) A child, including a foster child age 18 to 21, is eligible for an additional supplemental difficulty of care payment under subdivision 4, as determined by the assessment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607">142A.607</a>.</p>
          <p>(e) An eligible child entering Northstar kinship assistance or adoption assistance under the age of six receives a basic payment and supplemental difficulty of care payment as specified in subdivision 5.</p>
          <p>(f) A child transitioning in from a pre-Northstar Care for Children program under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.612#stat.142A.612.7">142A.612, subdivision 7</a>, shall receive basic and difficulty of care supplemental payments according to those provisions.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.609.2">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 2.<span class="headnote">Medical assistance.</span></h2>
          <p>Eligibility for medical assistance under this chapter must be determined according to section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256B.055">256B.055</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.609.3">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 3.<span class="headnote">Basic monthly rate.</span></h2>
          <p>From July 1, 2017, to June 30, 2018, the basic monthly rate must be according to the following schedule:</p>
          <table>
            <col style="width:24.031007751938%">
            <col style="width:37.984496124031%">
            <col style="width:37.984496124031%">
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Ages 0-5</td>
              <td> $650 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Ages 6-12</td>
              <td> $770 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Ages 13 and older</td>
              <td> $910 per month</td>
            </tr>
          </table>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.609.4">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 4.<span class="headnote">Difficulty of care supplemental monthly rate.</span></h2>
          <p>From January 1, 2015, to June 30, 2016, the supplemental difficulty of care monthly rate is determined by the following schedule:</p>
          <table>
            <col style="width:24.8%">
            <col style="width:28%">
            <col style="width:47.2%">
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td class="valign_top">Level A</td>
              <td class="valign_bottom">none </td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td class="valign_top">Level B</td>
              <td>none (basic under subdivision 3 only)</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level C</td>
              <td>$100 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level D</td>
              <td>$200 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level E</td>
              <td>$300 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level F</td>
              <td>$400 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level G</td>
              <td>$500 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level H</td>
              <td>$600 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level I</td>
              <td>$700 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level J</td>
              <td>$800 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level K</td>
              <td>$900 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level L</td>
              <td>$1,000 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level M</td>
              <td>$1,100 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level N</td>
              <td>$1,200 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level O</td>
              <td>$1,300 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level P</td>
              <td>$1,400 per month</td>
            </tr>
            <tr>
              <td></td>
              <td>Level Q</td>
              <td>$1,500 per month</td>
            </tr>
          </table>
          <p>A child assigned level A is not eligible for either the basic or supplemental difficulty of care payment, while a child assigned level B is not eligible for the supplemental difficulty of care payment but is eligible for the basic monthly rate under subdivision 3.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.609.5">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 5.<span class="headnote">Alternate rates for preschool entry and certain transitioned children.</span></h2>
          <p>A child who entered the Northstar kinship assistance or adoption assistance components of Northstar Care for Children while under the age of six shall receive 50 percent of the amount the child would otherwise be entitled to under subdivisions 3 and 4. The commissioner may also use the 50 percent rate for a child who was transitioned into those components through declaration of the commissioner under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/256N.28#stat.256N.28.7">256N.28, subdivision 7</a>.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.609.6">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 6.<span class="headnote">Emergency foster care rate for initial placement.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A child who enters foster care due to immediate custody by a police officer or court order, consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/260C.175">260C.175, subdivisions 1</a> and 2, or equivalent provision under tribal code, shall receive the emergency foster care rate for up to 30 days. The emergency foster care rate cannot be extended beyond 30 days of the child's placement.</p>
          <p>(b) For this payment rate to be applied, at least one of three conditions must apply:</p>
          <p>(1) the child's initial placement must be in foster care in Minnesota;</p>
          <p>(2) the child's previous placement was more than two years ago; or</p>
          <p>(3) the child's previous placement was for fewer than 30 days and an assessment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607">142A.607</a> was not completed by an agency under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607">142A.607</a>.</p>
          <p>(c) The emergency foster care rate consists of the appropriate basic monthly rate under subdivision 3 plus a difficulty of care supplemental monthly rate of level D under subdivision 4.</p>
          <p>(d) The emergency foster care rate ends under any of three conditions:</p>
          <p>(1) when an assessment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607">142A.607</a> is completed;</p>
          <p>(2) when the placement ends; or</p>
          <p>(3) after 30 days have elapsed.</p>
          <p>(e) The financially responsible agency, in consultation with the legally responsible agency, if different, may replace the emergency foster care rate at any time by completing an initial assessment on which a revised difficulty of care supplemental monthly rate would be based. Consistent with section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607#stat.142A.607.11">142A.607, subdivision 11</a>, the caregiver may request a reassessment in writing for an initial assessment to replace the emergency foster care rate. This written request would initiate an initial assessment under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607#stat.142A.607.6">142A.607, subdivision 6</a>. If the revised difficulty of care supplemental level based on the initial assessment is higher than level D, then the revised higher rate shall apply retroactively to the beginning of the placement. If the revised level is lower, the lower rate shall apply on the date the initial assessment was completed.</p>
          <p>(f) If a child remains in foster care placement for more than 30 days, the emergency foster care rate ends after the 30th day of placement and an assessment under this section must be completed.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.609.7">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 7.<span class="headnote">Daily rates.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) The commissioner shall establish prorated daily rates to the nearest cent for the monthly rates under subdivisions 3 to 6. Daily rates must be routinely used when a partial month is involved for foster care, Northstar kinship assistance, or adoption assistance.</p>
          <p>(b) A full month payment is permitted if a foster child is temporarily absent from the foster home if the brief absence does not exceed 14 days and the child's placement continues with the same caregiver.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.609.8">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 8.<span class="headnote">Revision.</span></h2>
          <p>By April 1, 2016, for fiscal year 2017, and by each succeeding April 1 for the subsequent fiscal year, the commissioner shall review and revise the rates under subdivisions 3 to 6 based on the United States Department of Agriculture, Estimates of the Cost of Raising a Child, published by the United States Department of Agriculture, Agricultural Resources Service, Publication 1411. The revision shall be the average percentage by which costs increase for the age ranges represented in the United States Department of Agriculture, Estimates of the Cost of Raising a Child, except that in no instance must the increase be more than three percent per annum. The monthly rates must be revised to the nearest dollar and the daily rates to the nearest cent.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.609.9">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 9.<span class="headnote">Home and vehicle modifications.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) Except for a child assigned level A under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.607#stat.142A.607.1">142A.607, subdivision 1</a>, paragraph (d), clause (1), a child who is eligible for an adoption assistance agreement may have reimbursement of home and vehicle modifications necessary to accommodate the child's special needs upon which eligibility for adoption assistance was based and included as part of the negotiation of the agreement under section <a href="https://www.revisor.mn.gov/statutes/cite/142A.608#stat.142A.608.3">142A.608, subdivision 3</a>. Reimbursement of home and vehicle modifications must not be available for a child who is assessed at level A under subdivision 1, unless and until the potential disability manifests itself and the agreement is renegotiated to include reimbursement.</p>
          <p>(b) Application for and reimbursement of modifications must be completed according to a process specified by the commissioner. The type and cost of each modification must be preapproved by the commissioner. The type of home and vehicle modifications must be limited to those specified by the commissioner.</p>
          <p>(c) Reimbursement for home modifications as outlined in this subdivision is limited to once every five years per child. Reimbursement for vehicle modifications as outlined in this subdivision is limited to once every five years per family.</p>
        </div>
        <div class="subd" id="stat.142A.609.10">
          
          <h2 class="subd_no">Subd. 10.<span class="headnote">Child income or income attributable to the child.</span></h2>
          <p>(a) A monthly Northstar kinship assistance or adoption assistance payment must be considered as income and resources attributable to the child. Northstar kinship assistance and adoption assistance are exempt from garnishment, except as permissible under the laws of the state where the child resides.</p>
